ORIGINAL RESEARCH published: 25 April 2019 doi: 10.3389/fpls.2019.00488 [SOURCE: Page_2]

Water Deficit-Responsive QTLs for Cell Wall Degradability and Composition in Maize at Silage Stage Laëtitia Virlouvet 1 , Fadi El Hage 1,2 , Yves Griveau 1 , Marie-Pierre Jacquemot 1 , Emilie Gineau 1 , Aurélie Baldy 1 , Sylvain Legay 1 , Christine Horlow 1 , Valérie Combes 3 , Cyril Bauland 3 , Carine Palafre 4 , Matthieu Falque 3 , Laurence Moreau 3 , Sylvie Coursol 1 , Valérie Méchin 1 and Matthieu Reymond 1* 1 Institut Jean-Pierre Bourgin, INRA, AgroParisTech, CNRS, Université Paris-Saclay, Versailles, France, 2 Univ. Paris-Sud, Université Paris-Saclay, Orsay, France, 3 Génétique Quantitative et Evolution - Le Moulon, INRA, Université Paris-Sud, CNRS, AgroParisTech, Université Paris-Saclay, Gif-sur-Yvette, France, 4 Unité Expérimentale du Maïs, INRA, Saint-Martin-de-Hinx, France [SOURCE: Page_2]

The use of lignocellulosic biomass for animal feed or biorefinery requires the optimization of its degradability. Moreover, biomass crops need to be better adapted to the changing climate and in particular to periods of drought. Although the negative impact of water deficit on biomass yield has often been mentioned, its impact on biomass quality has only been recently reported in a few species. In the present study, we combined Edited by:       the mapping power of a maize recombinant inbred line population with robust near Sebastien Christian Carpentier, Bioversity International, Belgium      infrared spectroscopy predictive equations to track the response to water deficit of traits Reviewed by:         associated with biomass quality. The population was cultivated under two contrasted Rogelio Santiago,        water regimes over 3 consecutive years in the south of France and harvested at University of Vigo, Spain Hamid Khazaei, silage stage. We showed that cell wall degradability and β-O-4-linked H lignin subunits University of Saskatchewan, Canada          were increased in response to water deficit, while lignin and p-coumaric acid contents *Correspondence:           were reduced. A mixed linear model was fitted to map quantitative trait loci (QTLs) for Matthieu Reymond          agronomical and cell wall-related traits. These QTLs were categorized as “constitutive” [EMAIL_REDACTED] (QTL with an effect whatever the irrigation condition) or “responsive” (QTL involved in Specialty section:       the response to water deficit) QTLs. Fifteen clusters of QTLs encompassed more than This article was submitted to      two third of the 213 constitutive QTLs and 13 clusters encompassed more than 60% of Plant Breeding, a section of the journal the 149 responsive QTLs. Interestingly, we showed that only half of the responsive QTLs Frontiers in Plant Science      co-localized with constitutive and yield QTLs, suggesting that specific genetic factors Received: 29 November 2018            support biomass quality response to water deficit. Overall, our results demonstrate that Accepted: 29 March 2019 water deficit favors cell wall degradability and that breeding of varieties that reconcile Published: 25 April 2019 improved drought-tolerance and biomass degradability is possible. Citation: Virlouvet L, El Hage F, Griveau Y,     Keywords: cell wall composition, cell wall degradability, drought response, quantitative trait locus, constitutive Jacquemot M-P, Gineau E, Baldy A,          QTL, responsive QTL, maize Legay S, Horlow C, Combes V, Bauland C, Palafre C, Falque M, Moreau L, Coursol S, Méchin V and Reymond M (2019) Water INTRODUCTION Deficit-Responsive QTLs for Cell Wall Degradability and Composition in        Besides its use as animal feed, lignocellulosic biomass is increasingly used for the production of Maize at Silage Stage.      second generation biofuel or chemical building blocks (Torres et al., 2015; Vermerris and Abril, Front. Plant Sci. 10:488.     2015; Bichot et al., 2018). Many of these uses require an efficient enzymatic degradation of the doi: 10.3389/fpls.2019.00488        biomass. Degradability is measured as the percentage of lignocellulosic biomass that is assimilated [SOURCE: Page_2]

FIGURE 2 | Summary of the clusters of constitutive and responsive QTLs identified for agronomic, cell wall composition, degradability, and PCA coordinate components traits. The color corresponds to the allele increasing the traits, F271 in magenta and Cm^484 in cyan. The scale of colors represented the effect at the QTL position (Supplemental Table 2) over the variation of the corresponding trait on the RIL progeny lines (see materials and methods). The symbols correspond to co-localization between constitutive and responsive QTLs. The references correspond to: [1] Méchin et al., 2001; [2] Roussel et al., 2002; [3] Cardinal et al., 2003; [4] Fontaine et al., 2003; [5] Krakowsky et al., 2005; [6] Krakowsky et al., 2006; [7] Riboulet et al., 2008a; [8] Barrière et al., 2008; [9] Wei et al., 2009; [10] Truntzler et al., 2010; [11] Lorenzana et al., 2010; [12] Barrière et al., 2012; [13] Courtial et al., 2013; [14] Torres et al., 2015; [15] Leng et al., 2018.
